# Community Medicine (PSM) — Multi-Year PYQ Pool

> **Source:** PrepLadder PYQ compilation + PrepLadder National Health Programs + PrepLadder Levels of Prevention + PrepLadder PSM Clinical/Non-Clinical + DigiNerve 2025 recall + pw.live PSM (student recall reconstructions).  
> **Use:** Personal study notes. ~220 questions spanning multiple years.

---

### Q1 — Sharp waste disposal bag colour

Needles and scalpel blades (sharps) are disposed in which coloured bag/container?

- A. Yellow bag
- B. Red bag
- C. Blue bag
- D. White/translucent puncture-proof container

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. White/translucent puncture-proof container** — Bio-Medical Waste Rules (India): sharps (needles, scalpels, broken glass) → white translucent puncture-proof container. Yellow = anatomical waste, soiled items. Red = recyclable plastic waste.
</details>

---

### Q2 — Factories Act 1948 maximum weekly work hours

Maximum weekly working hours including overtime under the Factories Act 1948?

- A. 48 hours
- B. 72 hours
- C. 60 hours
- D. 66 hours

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 60 hours** — Normal limit: 48 hours/week (9 hours/day). With overtime: maximum 60 hours/week and 50 hours overtime per quarter. Overtime is paid at double the ordinary rate.
</details>

---

### Q3 — Prospective screening example

Which of the following is an example of prospective screening?

- A. Cervical Pap smear in 40-year-old
- B. Neonatal hypothyroidism screening
- C. Screening of immigrants for tuberculosis
- D. Urine sugar in a clinic patient

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Screening of immigrants for infectious diseases** — Prospective screening: applied to population groups expected to enter a community (immigrants, recruits); screens before problems arise. Other options are mass/selective screening applied to existing populations.
</details>

---

### Q4 — Financial advantage quantification in health programmes

Which analysis measures programme outcomes in monetary terms?

- A. Cost-effectiveness analysis
- B. Cost-benefit analysis
- C. Network analysis
- D. Input-output analysis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cost-benefit analysis** — Converts both costs AND benefits to monetary units (rupees); calculates benefit-to-cost ratio. Cost-effectiveness analysis = compares cost per unit of health outcome (e.g., cost per DALY averted) without monetary conversion of outcomes.
</details>

---

### Q5 — Pneumonia in 24-month-old: management

24-month-old child diagnosed with pneumonia (no danger signs). Correct management?

- A. Oral ampicillin for 5 days
- B. Urgent tertiary referral
- C. IV antibiotics with 24-hour follow-up
- D. Oral amoxicillin for 5 days (with home care)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Oral amoxicillin for 5 days** — IMNCI/WHO guidelines: pneumonia in child 2 months–5 years without danger signs = oral amoxicillin 40 mg/kg/day × 5 days at home; fast breathing is key sign; return if worsening or not improving in 2 days.
</details>

---

### Q6 — Air velocity measurement instrument

Which instrument measures air velocity/movement?

- A. Kata thermometer
- B. Globe thermometer
- C. Wet globe thermometer
- D. Dial thermometer

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Kata thermometer** — Measures air velocity by timing how long it takes for the large alcohol bulb to cool from 38°C to 35°C; used in occupational settings. Anemometer is more modern air velocity tool. Globe thermometer = radiant heat.
</details>

---

### Q7 — Bubonic plague vector

Buboes (painful enlarged lymph nodes) are transmitted by which vector?

- A. Xenopsylla cheopis (oriental rat flea)
- B. Phlebotomus argentipes
- C. Ixodes tick
- D. Female Anopheles mosquito

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Xenopsylla cheopis** — Oriental rat flea; vector of Yersinia pestis (plague) from rat reservoir to humans. Phlebotomus = Leishmania; Ixodes = Lyme disease, RMSF; Anopheles = malaria.
</details>

---

### Q8 — Post-flood mosquito control chemical

Which chemical is used for mosquito control in post-flood conditions?

- A. Zinc phosphide
- B. Malathion
- C. Lindane
- D. Paris green

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Malathion** — Organophosphate insecticide; used for space spraying and residual spraying for mosquito control in disaster situations. Paris green = larval control (historical). DDT = residual spraying (restricted use). Zinc phosphide = rodenticide.
</details>

---

### Q9 — Hospital floor blood disinfection

Best disinfectant for blood-contaminated hospital floors?

- A. Ethyl alcohol
- B. Chlorhexidine
- C. Sodium hypochlorite (1%)
- D. Formaldehyde

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Sodium hypochlorite** — 1% NaOCl (10,000 ppm) for blood spills; effective against bacteria, viruses (including HIV, HBV), fungi. Recommended by WHO/CDC for environmental decontamination. Chlorhexidine = skin antiseptic (not floor disinfection).
</details>

---

### Q10 — Air Quality Index: AQI = 407

AQI value of 407 falls in which category?

- A. Moderately polluted
- B. Poor
- C. Very poor
- D. Severe

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Severe** — India's AQI scale: 0–50 = Good; 51–100 = Satisfactory; 101–200 = Moderate; 201–300 = Poor; 301–400 = Very Poor; 401–500 = Severe. AQI 407 = Severe (health emergency; avoid outdoor activities).
</details>

---

### Q11 — IMNCI dehydration classification

Child with drowsiness, poor feeding, slow skin retraction (pinch test > 2 seconds). IMNCI classification?

- A. Mild dehydration
- B. Some dehydration
- C. Severe dehydration
- D. Moderate dehydration

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Severe dehydration** — IMNCI: severe dehydration = lethargic/unconscious + sunken eyes + unable to drink/drinks poorly + skin pinch goes back very slowly (> 2 sec). Plan C = IV Ringer's lactate urgently.
</details>

---

### Q12 — QALY calculation

Japan female life expectancy 87 years; intervention adds 15 quality-adjusted years at utility 0.8. Metric used?

- A. HALE
- B. DALY
- C. DFLE
- D. QALY

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. QALY (Quality-Adjusted Life Year)** — QALY = life years gained × utility value (0–1); combines quantity and quality of life. 15 years × 0.8 = 12 QALYs. Used in cost-utility analysis for health economic decisions.
</details>

---

### Q13 — PHC disaster preparedness measures

Which disaster preparedness measures should a PHC adopt?

- A. Resource availability only
- B. Public awareness campaigns only
- C. Simulation drills only
- D. Resource availability + public awareness + simulation drills

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All three** — PHC disaster preparedness: (1) maintain drug/equipment stock, (2) community education, (3) regular mock drills/tabletop exercises to test response protocols. All are essential components of preparedness.
</details>

---

### Q14 — Most cost-effective rabies reduction strategy

Most cost-effective approach to reduce human rabies?

- A. Test all dogs for rabies
- B. Reduce stray dog population and vaccinate all dogs
- C. Increase laboratory capacity
- D. Increase passive surveillance

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Reduce stray dog population and vaccinate all dogs** — Dogs are the source of 99% of human rabies deaths; mass dog vaccination (≥ 70% coverage) interrupts transmission. More cost-effective than post-exposure prophylaxis alone. WHO target: zero dog-mediated human rabies by 2030.
</details>

---

### Q15 — WHO STEPS approach: excluded component

Which assessment is NOT part of the WHO STEPwise approach to NCD surveillance?

- A. Therapeutic assessment
- B. Physical measurements (Step 2)
- C. Biochemical measurements (Step 3)
- D. Behavioural assessment (Step 1)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Therapeutic assessment** — WHO STEPS: Step 1 = Behavioural (questionnaire); Step 2 = Physical measurements (BP, BMI, waist); Step 3 = Biochemical (blood glucose, cholesterol). Treatment/therapeutic data is NOT part of STEPS.
</details>

---

### Q16 — ITP most effective treatment

Most effective treatment for chronic/recurrent ITP?

- A. IV immunoglobulins
- B. Steroids
- C. Splenectomy
- D. Blood transfusion

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Splenectomy** — Spleen is primary site of platelet destruction (anti-platelet IgG-coated platelets phagocytosed by splenic macrophages) and autoantibody production; splenectomy gives durable remission in ~70%. IV IgG/steroids are for acute management/temporisation.
</details>

---

### Q17 — Malaria surveillance: annual blood examination rate

Most effective indicator for measuring malaria surveillance effectiveness?

- A. Annual Parasite Index (API)
- B. Annual Blood Examination Rate (ABER)
- C. Slide positivity rate (SPR)
- D. Slide falciparum rate (SFR)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Annual Blood Examination Rate (ABER)** — Measures the proportion of the population at risk that underwent blood examination for malaria in a year; best indicator of surveillance effort/quality. API = confirmed cases per 1000 population (measures disease burden, not surveillance effort).
</details>

---

### Q18 — Confounding: incorrect statement

Which is an INCORRECT statement about confounding factors?

- A. Can be reduced by matching in study design
- B. Associated with both the exposure and the outcome
- C. Distributed equally in both study and control groups
- D. Associated with the exposure

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Distributed equally in both groups** — A confounding variable is unequally distributed between study groups (that is what makes it a confounder and creates bias). If equally distributed, it cannot confound. Confounders are controlled by matching, stratification, or multivariate analysis.
</details>

---

### Q19 — Breast cancer prevention level (Leavell & Clark)

Mammography screening for early breast cancer represents which level of prevention?

- A. Specific protection
- B. Early diagnosis and prompt treatment
- C. Disability limitation
- D. Rehabilitation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Early diagnosis and prompt treatment (Secondary prevention)** — Screening detects pre-clinical disease → early treatment → better prognosis. Primary prevention = reduce incidence (e.g., reduce alcohol). Tertiary prevention = disability limitation, rehabilitation.
</details>

---

### Q20 — Vitamin A for keratomalacia: dosing schedule

Correct Vitamin A dosing for a child with keratomalacia?

- A. 200,000 IU immediately + repeat at 24 hours
- B. 100,000 IU immediately + 1 week later
- C. 100,000 IU at three intervals
- D. 200,000 IU immediately + 24 hours + 2 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 200,000 IU immediately + at 24 hours + at 2 weeks** — WHO schedule for Vit A deficiency with xerophthalmia/keratomalacia (children ≥ 12 months): 200,000 IU oral × 3 doses (Day 1, Day 2, Day 8–14). < 12 months: 100,000 IU per dose.
</details>

### Q21 — Chadah Committee: exception *(2018)*

The Chadah Committee recommended all of the following **except**:

- A. PHC at block level
- B. One PHC for every 50,000 population
- C. Basic health workers
- D. Responsibility in malaria eradication

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. One PHC for every 50,000 population** — Kartar Singh Committee (1973), not Chadah, recommended one PHC per 50,000. Chadah Committee (1963) recommended PHCs at block level, basic health workers, and malaria eradication responsibility.

</details>

### Q22 — RNTCP most peripheral unit *(2018)*

Most peripheral centre under the RNTCP structure?

- A. District TB Centre
- B. Intermediate Reference Laboratory
- C. Tuberculosis Unit
- D. Designated Microscopy Centre

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Designated Microscopy Centre** — RNTCP hierarchy (peripheral to central): Peripheral Health Institution → Designated Microscopy Centre → Tuberculosis Unit → District TB Centre → State TB Cell → Central TB Division.

</details>

### Q23 — Factories Act: true statement *(2018)*

Which is **true** about the Factories Act 1948?

- A. Children under 14 years must not be employed in factories
- B. A child of 16 years can be employed between 6 AM and 8 PM
- C. Maximum working hours is 72 hours per week
- D. A child of 16 years can work 5 hours per day

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Children under 14 years must not be employed** — Adolescents (14–18 years): may work 6 AM–7 PM, ≤4.5 h/day. Adult maximum: 48 h/week; with overtime: 60 h/week.

</details>

### Q24 — Iodine RDA for lactating women (NIN 2020) *(2018)*

Iodine RDA for lactating women per NIN 2020?

- A. 200 mcg/day
- B. 220 mcg/day
- C. 250 mcg/day
- D. 280 mcg/day

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 280 mcg/day** — NIN 2020: Adults = 150 mcg; Pregnancy = 250 mcg; Lactation = 280 mcg. WHO recommends 250 mcg for both pregnancy and lactation.

</details>

### Q25 — Non-parametric tests: true statement *(2018)*

Which is **true** for non-parametric tests?

- A. ANOVA is an example of a non-parametric test
- B. Used for skewed distributions
- C. Assume data follows a normal distribution
- D. Cannot be used for small sample sizes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Used for skewed distributions** — Non-parametric tests make no normality assumption; used for skewed data, ordinal data, or small samples. ANOVA is parametric. Chi-square is a common non-parametric test.

</details>

### Q26 — Right to Life: Article of Indian Constitution *(2018)*

Which article confers the Right to Life?

- A. Article 11
- B. Article 21
- C. Article 23
- D. Article 25

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Article 21** — "No person shall be deprived of his life or personal liberty except according to procedure established by law." Forms the legal basis for right to health.

</details>

### Q27 — WHO measles eradication coverage threshold *(2018)*

WHO-required immunisation coverage for measles eradication?

- A. 68%
- B. 72%
- C. 84%
- D. 96%

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 96%** — At least 96% immunisation coverage of children under 1 year needed for measles eradication, reflecting the high herd immunity threshold (R₀ ≈ 12–18).

</details>

### Q28 — Interpreting p-value (cancer remission study) *(2018)*

New treatment (33.2% remission) vs usual (32.2%), p = 0.04. Correct conclusion?

- A. Both equally effective
- B. Neither effective
- C. New treatment more effective
- D. Inadequate information

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. New treatment is more effective** — p < 0.05 → statistically significant → reject null hypothesis → accept alternative hypothesis (new treatment more effective).

</details>

### Q29 — ORS volume for some dehydration in infant (IMNCI Plan B) *(2018)*

9-month-old with diarrhoea, thirsty, skin pinch returns in 2 seconds. Treatment?

- A. 200–400 ml ORS in 4 hours
- B. 400–700 ml ORS in 4 hours
- C. 700–900 ml ORS in 4 hours
- D. 100–200 ml ORS after each loose stool (in addition to usual fluids)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 400–700 ml ORS in 4 hours** — Some dehydration → IMNCI Plan B: 75 ml/kg × ~9 kg ≈ 675 ml over 4 hours.

</details>

### Q30 — Lethal BMI in men *(2018)*

BMI value considered lethal in men?

- A. <12
- B. <13
- C. <14
- D. <15

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. <13** — BMI ≤13 kg/m² is lethal in men. Women can survive to BMI ~11 kg/m² due to different body composition.

</details>

### Q31 — Relative risk definition *(2018)*

Ratio of incidence in exposed to incidence in non-exposed?

- A. Attributable risk
- B. Odds ratio
- C. Relative risk
- D. Population attributable risk

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Relative risk** — RR = incidence (exposed) ÷ incidence (non-exposed). Derived from cohort studies. RR >1 = positive association; <1 = protective.

</details>

### Q32 — CPR threshold for NRR = 1 *(2018)*

To achieve NRR = 1, couple protection rate (CPR) must exceed:

- A. 50%
- B. 40%
- C. 70%
- D. 60%

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 60%** — CPR >60% achieves population replacement-level fertility (NRR = 1, TFR ≈ 2.1).

</details>

### Q33 — NIKSHAY vs NIKUSHTH *(2018)*

Online software under NTEP to monitor TB patients?

- A. NIKSHAY
- B. NISCHAY
- C. E-DOTS
- D. NIKUSHTH

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. NIKSHAY** — NIKSHAY = TB patient monitoring (NTEP). NIKUSHTH = leprosy patient monitoring.

</details>

### Q34 — Cytotoxic drug disposal *(2018)*

Cytotoxic and expired drugs are disposed of by:

- A. Dumping
- B. Autoclaving
- C. Incineration
- D. Chemical disinfection

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Incineration** — Yellow bag → incineration (or plasma pyrolysis). Other methods apply to different waste categories.

</details>

### Q35 — Sensitivity calculation *(2018)*

Screening test: 90 positive out of 1000; gold standard identifies 100 positives. Sensitivity?

- A. 90/100
- B. 100/110
- C. (90−10)/1000
- D. 90/1000

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 90/100 = 90%** — Sensitivity = TP/(TP+FN) = 90/(90+10) = 90%. FN = 100 − 90 = 10.

</details>

### Q36 — Prevalence formula *(2018)*

Incidence 4/1000, average duration 2 years. Prevalence?

- A. 8/100
- B. 2/1000
- C. 4/1000
- D. 8/1000

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 8/1000** — Prevalence = Incidence × Mean Duration = 4/1000 × 2 = 8/1000.

</details>

### Q37 — Secondary attack rate (SAR) *(2018)*

Probability of infection in susceptibles following contact within the incubation period?

- A. Secondary attack rate
- B. Case fatality rate
- C. Primary attack rate
- D. Tertiary attack rate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Secondary attack rate (SAR)** — SAR = (susceptibles developing disease within incubation period × 100) ÷ total susceptible contacts. Primary cases excluded.

</details>

### Q38 — Water hardness: not caused by *(2018)*

Which does **not** cause hardness of water?

- A. Calcium carbonate
- B. Calcium sulphate
- C. Calcium bicarbonate
- D. Magnesium sulphate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Calcium carbonate** — Insoluble, precipitates out; does not dissolve to cause hardness. Hardness caused by bicarbonates (temporary) and sulphates/chlorides/nitrates (permanent) of Ca²⁺ and Mg²⁺.

</details>

### Q39 — Droplet nuclei: direct or indirect transmission? *(2018)*

Which is **not** a method of direct transmission?

- A. Vertical transmission
- B. Contact with soil
- C. Droplet nuclei
- D. Droplet transmission

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Droplet nuclei** — Droplet nuclei (≤5 µm) = indirect/airborne transmission. They remain suspended in air. Direct transmission includes droplets (>5 µm), vertical transmission, contact.

</details>

### Q40 — Minimum interval between two live vaccines *(2018)*

Minimum interval between two different live vaccines?

- A. 2 weeks
- B. 4 weeks
- C. 8 weeks
- D. 12 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 4 weeks** — If not given simultaneously, a minimum 4-week interval between two different live vaccines prevents immune interference.

</details>

### Q41 — Kala-Azar endemic states *(2018)*

Kala-Azar is endemic in all states **except**:

- A. West Bengal
- B. Uttar Pradesh
- C. Bihar
- D. Assam

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Assam** — Kala-Azar endemic in 54 districts: Bihar, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Uttar Pradesh. Assam is NOT endemic.

</details>

### Q42 — Ecological study: unit of observation *(2018)*

Unit of observation in ecological studies?

- A. Population
- B. Patient
- C. Healthy individual
- D. Case

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Population** — Ecological studies analyse aggregate (population-level) data, not individual data. Ecological fallacy = applying group-level associations to individuals.

</details>

### Q43 — DTwP vaccine: false statement *(2019)*

Which statement is **false** about the DTwP vaccine?

- A. Administered on anterolateral aspect of thigh
- B. Prior adverse reaction with temperature >37°C is a contraindication
- C. Cellular pertussis not recommended after 7 years
- D. Diphtheria toxoid dose in adults is lesser than paediatric dose

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Fever >37°C is a contraindication** — FALSE. Temperature >40.5°C, seizures, and HHE are **precautions**, not contraindications. 37°C is not a threshold for any contraindication.

</details>

### Q44 — ABER: malaria surveillance efficiency *(2019)*

Parameter measuring **efficiency** of malaria surveillance under NVBDCP?

- A. Annual Parasite Index
- B. Annual Blood Examination Rate
- C. Slide positivity rate
- D. Slide falciparum rate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Annual Blood Examination Rate (ABER)** — ABER measures what proportion of the population had blood smears examined in a year. Target ≥10%. API measures incidence (cases/1000 population).

</details>

### Q45 — Confounding factor: false statement *(2019)*

Which is **false** about a confounding factor?

- A. Can be reduced by matching
- B. Associated individually with both exposure and outcome
- C. Distributed equally in both study and control groups
- D. Associated with the exposure under study

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Distributed equally in both groups** — FALSE. Confounders are distributed **unequally** between groups — that is why they distort the association. Control methods: matching, randomisation, stratification, multivariable analysis.

</details>

### Q46 — Best prevention level for breast cancer *(2019)*

Best level of prevention of breast cancer?

- A. Specific protection
- B. Early diagnosis and treatment
- C. Disability limitation
- D. Rehabilitation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Early diagnosis and treatment** — Secondary prevention. Breast cancer lacks effective specific protection. Mammography screening reduces mortality.

</details>

### Q47 — Vitamin A dosage for keratomalacia (2-year-old) *(2019)*

Vitamin A dosage for a 2-year-old with keratomalacia?

- A. 2,00,000 IU immediately + 24 hours only
- B. 1,00,000 IU immediately + 1 week
- C. 1,00,000 IU immediately + 2 days + 1 week
- D. 2,00,000 IU immediately + 24 hours + 2 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 2,00,000 IU immediately + 24 hours + 2 weeks** — WHO schedule for children ≥12 months with clinical VAD: 2,00,000 IU on Day 1, Day 2, and Day 8–14.

</details>

### Q48 — Normal distribution: percentage within ±1 SD *(2019)*

Percentage of values within ±1 SD in a normal distribution?

- A. 68%
- B. 34%
- C. 99%
- D. 95%

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 68%** — Empirical rule: ±1 SD = 68%; ±2 SD = 95%; ±3 SD = 99.7%.

</details>

### Q49 — IMNCI: fast breathing threshold in 6-month-old *(2019)*

IMNCI defines pneumonia in a 6-month-old as RR >:

- A. 40/min
- B. 60/min
- C. 30/min
- D. 50/min

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 50/min** — IMNCI: <2 months = >60/min; 2–11 months = >50/min; 12 months–5 years = >40/min.

</details>

### Q50 — Concurrent List: Indian Constitution *(2019)*

Which is in the **Concurrent List**?

- A. Adulteration of food
- B. Fishing beyond territorial waters
- C. Regulating labour in mines
- D. Public health and sanitation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Adulteration of food** — Both central and state governments can legislate. Public health and sanitation are in the State List.

</details>

### Q51 — Principal unit of administration in India *(2019)*

The principal unit of administration in India is the:

- A. Village
- B. Town
- C. District
- D. State

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. District** — Headed by the District Collector (IAS). The district is the principal administrative unit.

</details>

### Q52 — Odds ratio: which study design? *(2019)*

Cross-product ratio (odds ratio) is obtained from:

- A. Ecological study
- B. Cohort study
- C. Cross-sectional study
- D. Case-control study

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Case-control study** — OR = ad/bc. OR ≈ RR for rare diseases. RR = cohort studies.

</details>

### Q53 — Trench method land requirement *(2019)*

Land required for the trench method for a population of 10,000?

- A. 4 acres
- B. 5 acres
- C. 2 acres
- D. 1 acre

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 5 acres** — Controlled tipping/sanitary landfill using trench method: approximately 5 acres/year for 10,000 population.

</details>

### Q54 — Unpaired T test: two-group mean comparison *(2019)*

Best test to measure difference in means between two independent groups (one = control)?

- A. Chi-square
- B. Z test
- C. Unpaired T test
- D. Paired T test

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Unpaired T test** — Unpaired T = two independent groups. Paired T = same group before/after. Chi-square = categorical data. ANOVA = ≥3 groups.

</details>

### Q55 — Chi-square test: proportions *(2019)*

Test of significance for two or more proportions?

- A. Chi-square test
- B. Student's T test
- C. Z test
- D. ANOVA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Chi-square test** — Non-parametric; for categorical/nominal data and proportions. ANOVA and T tests compare quantitative means.

</details>

### Q56 — Positive predictive value *(2019)*

Probability of truly having the disease given a positive test?

- A. Sensitivity
- B. Specificity
- C. PPV
- D. NPV

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. PPV** — PPV = TP/(TP+FP). Increases with disease prevalence. Sensitivity and specificity are fixed test properties, independent of prevalence.

</details>

### Q57 — Lead time definition *(2019)*

Period from first possible detection to usual time of clinical diagnosis?

- A. Screening time
- B. Lead time
- C. Generation time
- D. Incubation period

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Lead time** — Lead time bias = earlier detection only appears to improve survival; does not necessarily change natural history.

</details>

### Q58 — Component bar graph *(2019)*

Statistical diagram showing total population change divided into components over years?

- A. Simple bar graph
- B. Component bar graph
- C. Multiple bar graph
- D. Frequency polygon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Component bar graph** — Stacked bar showing subsets/components within total magnitude. For nominal/ordinal data with subcategories.

</details>

### Q59 — Triage: lowest priority *(2019)*

True statement about disaster-site triage?

- A. First-come, first-served
- B. Green = priority transport
- C. Moribund patients get lowest priority
- D. Three-colour code most common

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Moribund patients get lowest priority** — Black/expectant category: survival unlikely even with maximum care. Four-colour system (START): Red/Yellow/Green/Black is most common.

</details>

### Q60 — Consanguinity and genetic disease risk *(2019)*

Risk of genetic disease in first-cousin consanguineous marriage?

- A. 1–2%
- B. 4–8%
- C. 8–10%
- D. 12–14%

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 4–8%** — First cousins share 1/8 of genes (coefficient of relationship = 0.125). Non-consanguineous baseline = 2–4%.

</details>

### Q61 — Paradoxical carrier *(2019)*

Paradoxical carrier = acquired pathogen from:

- A. Sheds during incubation period
- B. Another carrier
- C. A patient
- D. Becomes carrier after recovery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Another carrier** — Paradoxical carrier acquired the organism from a carrier (not a clinical case). Incubatory carrier = sheds during incubation. Convalescent = sheds after recovery.

</details>

### Q62 — Vaccine contraindicated in pregnancy *(2019)*

Which vaccine is contraindicated in pregnancy?

- A. Hepatitis A
- B. Hepatitis B
- C. Rabies
- D. Varicella

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Varicella** — Live attenuated vaccine; risk of fetal varicella syndrome. Hepatitis A, B, and rabies (inactivated) are safe in pregnancy.

</details>

### Q63 — Lab coat: not a PPE *(2019)*

Which is **not** a PPE?

- A. Goggles
- B. Gloves
- C. Face shield
- D. Lab coat

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Lab coat** — Not classified as PPE. Standard PPE: gloves, gown/apron, mask/respirator, goggles, face shield.

</details>

### Q64 — Line graph for disease incidence trends *(2019)*

Best diagram to show changes in disease incidence over time?

- A. Line graph
- B. Histogram
- C. Ogive
- D. Scatter diagram

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Line graph** — Line graphs display temporal trends. Histogram = frequency distribution. Ogive = cumulative frequency. Scatter = correlation.

</details>

### Q65 — Rubella: false statement *(2019)*

Which is **false** about Rubella infection?

- A. Droplet infection
- B. Vertical transmission possible
- C. Early pregnancy infection causes mild fetal disease
- D. Late pregnancy infection may cause only deafness

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Early pregnancy → mild fetal disease** — FALSE. First-trimester infection → most severe CRS: abortions, stillbirth, cataracts, heart defects, deafness, microcephaly, blueberry muffin rash. Later infection → less severe manifestations (D is TRUE).

</details>

### Q66 — HIV PEP after needlestick *(2019)*

Best HIV PEP regimen after needlestick from HIV-positive patient?

- A. Zidovudine + Lamivudine × 4 weeks
- B. Lamivudine + Tenofovir + Efavirenz × 4 weeks
- C. Tenofovir + Lamivudine + Dolutegravir × 4 weeks
- D. Zidovudine + Lamivudine + Nevirapine × 4 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. TDF + 3TC + DTG × 28 days** — NACO-recommended triple therapy for HIV PEP. Initiate within 2 hours (max 72 hours). HIV test at baseline and 3 months.

</details>

### Q67 — Sharp waste disposal container *(2020)*

Sharp waste is disposed of in:

- A. Yellow bag
- B. Red bag
- C. Blue container
- D. White puncture-proof container

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. White puncture-proof container** — BMW Rules 2016: Sharps → white translucent puncture-proof, leak-proof container.

</details>

### Q68 — Factories Act: maximum weekly hours with overtime *(2020)*

Maximum work hours per week **including overtime** under the Factories Act 1948?

- A. 48 hours
- B. 72 hours
- C. 60 hours
- D. 66 hours

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 60 hours** — Normal = 48 h/week; with overtime (double rate) = max 60 h/week.

</details>

### Q69 — Mission Indradhanush *(2020)*

Launched to ensure complete immunisation of children?

- A. Mission Dhanush
- B. Mission Indradhanush
- C. Mission Indira Yojana
- D. Mission Suraksha Yojana

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Mission Indradhanush** — Launched 2014 by MoHFW; >90% coverage target. Intensified Mission Indradhanush (IMI) 2017. Currently covers 12 vaccine-preventable diseases.

</details>

### Q70 — Prospective screening: example *(2020)*

Example of **prospective screening**?

- A. Cervical Pap smear in 40-year-old
- B. Neonatal screening for hypothyroidism
- C. Screening of immigrants to a country
- D. Urine for sugar in 40-year-old man

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Screening of immigrants** — Prospective screening = done for the benefit of **others** (population protection from infectious disease). Individual screening (Pap smear, neonatal screen) benefits the individual.

</details>

### Q71 — Cost-benefit analysis *(2020)*

Monetary benefit is measured in:

- A. Cost-effectiveness analysis
- B. Cost-benefit analysis
- C. Network analysis
- D. Input-output analysis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cost-benefit analysis** — Both costs and benefits expressed in monetary terms. Cost-effectiveness = cost per unit health gain (e.g., cost/DALY averted).

</details>

### Q72 — IMNCI: pneumonia with chest indrawing, 2-year-old *(2020)*

24-month-old, RR 38/min, chest indrawing — next step?

- A. Oral amoxicillin for 5 days
- B. Urgent referral to tertiary care
- C. Antibiotics + review at 24 hours
- D. Antipyretics urgently

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Oral amoxicillin for 5 days** — Revised IMNCI: chest indrawing WITHOUT danger signs = pneumonia → outpatient oral amoxicillin 5 days. Referral only if danger signs present.

</details>

### Q73 — ESI: extended sickness benefit for TB *(2020)*

Under ESI Act, extended sickness benefit for TB?

- A. 1 year
- B. 2 years
- C. 3 years
- D. 6 months

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 2 years** — Extended sickness benefit up to 2 years at 70% of wages for TB, mental illness, malignancy, and other long-duration illnesses.

</details>

### Q74 — Management by objective: based on which science? *(2020)*

Management by objective is based on:

- A. Decision making
- B. Systems analysis
- C. Network analysis
- D. Behavioural sciences

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Behavioural sciences** — MBO is a behavioural-science-based management method. Quantitative methods: PERT/CPM, cost-benefit analysis, input-output analysis.

</details>

### Q75 — Chi-square: nutritional status in two areas *(2020)*

30 rural and 20 urban children malnourished — most appropriate test?

- A. Chi-square test
- B. Paired T test
- C. Standard error of mean
- D. ANOVA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Chi-square test** — Comparing proportions (malnourished/not) in two independent groups = Chi-square (2×2 categorical table).

</details>

### Q76 — Metabolic syndrome: NOT a NCEP-ATP III criterion *(2020)*

Not a criterion for metabolic syndrome per NCEP-ATP III?

- A. Hypertriglyceridaemia
- B. High LDL cholesterol
- C. Central obesity
- D. Hypertension

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. High LDL** — NCEP-ATP III criteria (3 of 5): waist circumference, triglycerides ≥150, low HDL, BP ≥130/85, FBG ≥100. LDL is NOT included.

</details>

### Q77 — Normal distribution: range for 68% *(2020)*

Mean = 200, SD = 20. 68% of population lies between:

- A. 180–220
- B. 160–240
- C. 170–230
- D. 190–210

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 180–220** — Mean ± 1SD = 200 ± 20 = 180–220 (68%). ±2SD = 160–240 (95%). ±3SD = 140–260 (99.7%).

</details>

### Q78 — Blood bag disposal colour *(2020)*

Blood bags are disposed of in:

- A. Yellow bag
- B. Red bag
- C. White container
- D. Blue bin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Yellow bag** — BMW 2016: Yellow = anatomical waste, soiled dressings, blood bags, expired blood, cytotoxic drugs. Red = plastic recyclables.

</details>

### Q79 — Coefficient of regression *(2020)*

Predicting one variable from another is done by:

- A. Coefficient of correlation
- B. Coefficient of regression
- C. Coefficient of variation
- D. Standard deviation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Coefficient of regression** — Regression predicts the dependent variable value from the independent variable. Correlation measures strength of relationship.

</details>

### Q80 — Air pollution monitoring indicators *(2020)*

Best indicators for routine air pollution monitoring?

- A. SO₂, smoke, suspended particulate matter
- B. CO and NO₂
- C. SO₂ and CO
- D. CO and H₂S

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. SO₂, smoke, and suspended particulate matter** — Standard routine ambient air quality indicators. Recommended by WHO and Park's PSM.

</details>

### Q81 — Ecological study: smoking and lung cancer *(2020)*

Researcher collects data on lung cancer deaths and cigarette packets sold in the same period. Study type?

- A. Cross-sectional
- B. Ecological
- C. Experimental
- D. Cohort

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Ecological** — Unit of analysis = population (aggregate data, not individual). Prone to ecological fallacy.

</details>

### Q82 — Project MONICA *(2020)*

Project MONICA is related to:

- A. Cardiovascular diseases
- B. Cervical cancer
- C. Breast cancer
- D. Road traffic accidents

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cardiovascular diseases** — MONICA = Multinational MOnitoring of trends and determinants In CArdiovascular disease (WHO). Studied MI, stroke, and risk factors across multiple countries.

</details>

### Q83 — Bleaching powder: disinfecting compound *(2020)*

Compound predominantly responsible for disinfection by bleaching powder?

- A. Hypochlorite ion
- B. Hydrochloric acid
- C. Hypochlorous acid
- D. Chloride ion

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Hypochlorous acid (HOCl)** — 70–80× more effective than hypochlorite ion. Bleaching powder = chlorinated lime with ~33% available chlorine.

</details>

### Q84 — Attributable risk *(2020)*

Difference between incidence in exposed and non-exposed?

- A. Relative risk
- B. Attributable risk
- C. Population attributable risk
- D. Odds ratio

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Attributable risk** — AR = Incidence (exposed) − Incidence (non-exposed). Absolute excess risk due to exposure. PAR = Incidence (total) − Incidence (non-exposed).

</details>

### Q85 — SDG 3.1: MMR target by 2030 *(2020)*

SDG 3.1 target for maternal mortality ratio by 2030?

- A. <70 per 100,000 live births
- B. <100
- C. <50
- D. <130

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. <70 per 100,000 live births** — SDG 3.1 target by 2030. No country >140/100,000. India MMR (2020) ≈ 97/100,000.

</details>

### Q86 — Tetanus prophylaxis: clean wound, last dose 10 years ago *(2020)*

Clean wound, last tetanus dose 10 years ago — management?

- A. Full course of vaccine
- B. Single dose tetanus toxoid
- C. Tetanus toxoid + immunoglobulin
- D. No vaccine needed

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Single dose tetanus toxoid** — Clean wound + fully immunised + >10 years → 1 booster Td only. No TIG needed for clean wounds.

</details>

### Q87 — Coefficient of variation: comparing datasets *(2020)*

Comparing variation between two different datasets?

- A. Variance
- B. Coefficient of variation
- C. Standard error of mean
- D. Standard deviation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Coefficient of variation** — CV = (SD/Mean) × 100%. Unitless; allows comparison between datasets with different units or scales.

</details>

### Q88 — 2009 pandemic influenza *(2020)*

2009 pandemic caused by which influenza virus?

- A. H1N1
- B. H5N1
- C. H5N7
- D. H3N2

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. H1N1** — Influenza A (H1N1)pdm09 "Swine flu." Previous pandemics: 1918 = H1N1; 1957 = H2N2; 1968 = H3N2.

</details>

### Q89 — Kata thermometer: current use *(2021)*

Kata thermometer is currently used to measure:

- A. Cooling power of air
- B. Wet bulb temperature
- C. Radiant heat
- D. Low air velocity

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Low air velocity** — Originally measured cooling power of air; now used as anemometer for low air velocities. Dry kata ≥6 + wet kata ≥20 were thermal comfort indices.

</details>

### Q90 — Bubonic plague vector *(2021)*

Vector for bubonic plague outbreak?

- A. Xenopsylla cheopis
- B. Phlebotomus argentipes
- C. Ixodes tick
- D. Female Anopheles

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Xenopsylla cheopis** — Rat flea; vector for *Yersinia pestis*. *Phlebotomus argentipes* = kala-azar. *Ixodes* = Lyme disease.

</details>

### Q91 — Leptospirosis flood control chemical *(2021)*

Given with doxycycline during floods for disease control in Kerala?

- A. Zinc phosphide
- B. Malathion
- C. Lindane
- D. Paris green

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Malathion** — Vector control insecticide distributed during floods. Doxycycline 200 mg/week = leptospirosis chemoprophylaxis.

</details>

### Q92 — Blood spill disinfectant *(2021)*

Blood spillage on hospital floor — disinfectant of choice?

- A. Ethyl alcohol
- B. Chlorhexidine
- C. Sodium hypochlorite
- D. Formaldehyde

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Sodium hypochlorite 0.5%** — Blood/body fluid spill → 0.5% NaOCl (1:10 dilution of 5% bleach). Absorb → clean with detergent → saturate with NaOCl for 30 min.

</details>

### Q93 — AQI 394–407: classification *(2021)*

AQI of ~394–407 is classified as:

- A. Moderate (101–200)
- B. Poor (201–300)
- C. Very poor (301–400)
- D. Severe (401–500)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Very poor (301–400)** — India's CPCB AQI: Good ≤50, Satisfactory 51–100, Moderate 101–200, Poor 201–300, Very poor 301–400, Severe 401–500.

</details>

### Q94 — Japanese Encephalitis vaccine *(2021)*

JE vaccine: type and route used in outbreak drive?

- A. Live, subcutaneous
- B. Killed, intramuscular
- C. Live, intramuscular
- D. Killed, subcutaneous

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Killed, intramuscular** — Inactivated JE vaccine is given IM. (The SA 14-14-2 live attenuated vaccine used in NIP for children is given SC; the killed IM form is referenced for adult/outbreak scenarios.)

</details>

### Q95 — Process evaluation *(2021)*

Evaluation of treatment given and clinical management in a facility measures?

- A. Outcome
- B. Process
- C. Structure
- D. Input

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Process** — Donabedian's triad: Structure (resources) → Process (what is done) → Outcome (results). Evaluating treatment given = process.

</details>

### Q96 — MMR contraindicated in immunocompromised child *(2021)*

Vaccine contraindicated in a child with primary immunodeficiency?

- A. IPV
- B. MMR
- C. DPT
- D. TT/Td

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. MMR** — Live attenuated vaccine; contraindicated in immunocompromised patients. Inactivated vaccines (IPV, DPT, TT) are safe.

</details>

### Q97 — Conditioning influence for malnutrition *(2021)*

Conditioning influence for malnutrition?

- A. Child-rearing practices
- B. Infectious diseases
- C. Food habits
- D. Genetic factors

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Infectious diseases** — Immediate/conditioning influence → precipitates malnutrition by increasing metabolic demand and reducing absorption. Diarrhoea, ARI, and measles are key triggers.

</details>

### Q98 — CRP and MI/stroke risk *(2021)*

Study shows increasing CRP quintiles with increasing RR for MI/stroke. Correct statement?

- A. No association
- B. Higher CRP → higher MI/stroke risk
- C. Higher CRP → lower risk
- D. Quintile 1 has no risk

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Increase in CRP increases the risk of MI/stroke** — Dose-response relationship (higher CRP → higher RR). RR >1 = positive association. CRP is a marker of systemic inflammation and cardiovascular risk.

</details>

### Q99 — Throat swab disposal bin *(2021)*

Used throat swab for culture should be discarded in:

- A. Red
- B. Yellow
- C. White
- D. Blue

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Yellow** — Microbiology/clinical laboratory waste → yellow bag (autoclave-safe).

</details>

### Q100 — Candela: SI unit *(2021)*

SI unit for brightness of light from a point source?

- A. Candela
- B. Lux
- C. Lambert
- D. Lumen

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Candela** — SI base unit of luminous intensity. Lux = illuminance (lm/m²). Lumen = total luminous flux.

</details>

### Q101 — Varicella susceptibility: seronegative pregnancy *(2021)*

Pregnant woman exposed to varicella, serum antibodies negative — implies?

- A. Immune to zoster
- B. Susceptible to zoster
- C. Susceptible to chickenpox
- D. Immune to chickenpox

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Susceptible to chickenpox** — Negative IgG = no prior infection = susceptible to primary varicella. Offer VZIG within 96 hours.

</details>

### Q102 — School health: managed by which facility? *(2021)*

School health programmes are managed by:

- A. PHC
- B. District hospital
- C. Sub-centre
- D. Sub-divisional hospital

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Primary health centre (PHC)** — School health is one of PHC's 12 comprehensive primary healthcare packages.

</details>

### Q103 — Neurolathyrism: elicit which history? *(2021)*

Progressive motor paresis in man from Chhattisgarh — most important history?

- A. Family history
- B. Fever
- C. Joint pain
- D. Diet history

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Diet history** — Neurolathyrism from *Lathyrus sativus* (khesari dal). Diet history confirms consumption. Presents as pure spastic paraparesis (UMN disease) without sensory loss.

</details>

### Q104 — TT/Td at 10 years: school immunisation *(2021)*

Vaccine given to 10-year-old school child?

- A. BCG
- B. MMR
- C. TT/Td
- D. DPT

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. TT/Td** — National Immunisation Schedule: TT/Td at 10 years and 16 years as school-age boosters.

</details>

### Q105 — QALY calculation *(2022)*

Life expectancy gain 1.5 years, utility value 0.8 — which measure can be calculated?

- A. HALE
- B. DALY
- C. YLD
- D. QALY

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. QALY** — QALY = life years gained × utility value = 1.5 × 0.8 = 1.2 QALYs. Used in cost-utility analysis.

</details>

### Q106 — Disaster preparedness steps *(2022)*

Steps part of disaster **preparedness** (not response)?

1. Ensure resources available
2. Public awareness campaigns
3. Simulation exercises
4. Follow phone instructions during an event

- A. 1, 2, 3, 4
- B. 1 and 3
- C. 1, 2, and 3
- D. 2 and 4

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Steps 1, 2, and 3** — Step 4 (following instructions during event) = response, not preparedness. Preparedness activities occur before the disaster.

</details>

### Q107 — Rabies control: most cost-effective strategy *(2022)*

Most cost-effective strategy to reduce rabies incidence?

- A. Test all dogs for rabies
- B. Reduce stray dog population + vaccinate all dogs
- C. Increase laboratory facilities
- D. Vaccinate healthcare workers

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Reduce stray dog population + vaccinate dogs** — Source elimination + reservoir vaccination. India's ABC programme targets stray dog sterilisation and vaccination.

</details>

### Q108 — WHO STEPS approach: not included *(2022)*

Not included in WHO STEP approach for NCD surveillance?

- A. Therapeutic assessment
- B. Physical assessment
- C. Psychological assessment
- D. Behavioural assessment

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Therapeutic assessment** — STEPS: Step 1 = Behavioural; Step 2 = Physical; Step 3 = Biochemical. Therapeutic assessment is NOT a component.

</details>

### Q109 — TB treatment outcome: sputum negative at end *(2022)*

Sputum smear negative at end of full TB treatment = treatment outcome?

- A. Cured
- B. Treatment completed
- C. Lost to follow-up
- D. Treatment failed

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cured** — Cured = bacteriological confirmation + negative smear/culture at end of treatment. Treatment completed = finished treatment without bacteriological result.

</details>

### Q110 — RR < 1: protective association *(2022)*

Green tea vs no-tea cohort study for DM; risk ratio = 0.84. Correct statement?

- A. Green tea reduces DM risk
- B. Green tea increases DM risk
- C. Data insufficient
- D. 0.84 close to 1, hence no effect

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Green tea reduces DM risk** — RR = 0.84 < 1 = protective/inverse association. Statistically significant → green tea is protective against diabetes.

</details>

### Q111 — U-CHC: criteria for establishment *(2022)*

Best criterion for starting an Urban Community Health Centre?

- A. Population of 1–1.5 lakh
- B. Referral for 2–3 U-PHCs
- C. No sub-district or district hospitals present in area
- D. 100-bed facility in metro cities

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. No sub-district and district hospitals present** — U-CHC = 30–50 beds, caters to 2,50,000. Established to fill the referral gap where district hospitals are absent.

</details>

### Q112 — Broken vaccine vial disposal *(2022)*

Broken vaccine vial disposal per BMW Rules?

- A. Blue bin
- B. White container
- C. Yellow container
- D. Red container

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Blue bin** — Broken/discarded glass (vials, ampoules, slides) → blue bin. Sent for recycling after decontamination.

</details>

### Q113 — JSY: urban benefit in MP (Low Performing State) *(2022)*

JSY benefits for primigravida, institutional delivery, urban Madhya Pradesh?

- A. ₹1000 (mother) + ₹400 (ASHA)
- B. ₹1400 (mother) + ₹600 (ASHA)
- C. ₹600 (mother) + ₹400 (ASHA)
- D. ₹400 (mother) + ₹200 (ASHA)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. ₹1000 for mother + ₹400 for ASHA** — LPS (MP, UP, Bihar, etc.) urban: ₹1000 mother + ₹400 ASHA. LPS rural: ₹1400 + ₹600. HPS urban: ₹600 + ₹400.

</details>

### Q114 — UNICEF: seeds and manure in applied nutrition *(2022)*

Which agency provides seeds and manure in school applied nutrition programmes?

- A. CARE
- B. UNDP
- C. UNICEF
- D. WHO

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. UNICEF** — UNICEF provides implements, seeds, manure, water equipment for school gardens in the Applied Nutrition Programme. Also implemented GOBI-FFF strategy.

</details>

### Q115 — Case-control: controls must be disease-free *(2022)*

Controls in a case-control study for ALL investigation?

- A. Exposed area children without disease
- B. Unexposed area children with disease
- C. OPD children without disease
- D. All children with ALL

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. OPD children without disease** — Controls must be free from the disease (ALL) under study. Selected from same base population as cases.

</details>

### Q116 — Open vial policy *(2022)*

ANM has open pentavalent and MR vials at a camp — what can she do?

- A. Use MR, discard pentavalent
- B. Use pentavalent, discard MR
- C. Use both
- D. Discard both

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Use pentavalent, discard MR** — OVP: non-live multi-dose vaccines (pentavalent, DPT, Hep B, TT, OPV) can be reused. Live vaccines (MR, BCG, measles) must be discarded at session end once opened/reconstituted.

</details>

### Q117 — Neurolathyrism: prevention vitamin *(2023)*

Scissors gait in farmers eating rice and pulse diet; which vitamin could prevent this?

- A. Vitamin A
- B. Vitamin D
- C. Vitamin C
- D. Vitamin B complex

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Vitamin C** — Neurolathyrism from *Lathyrus sativus* (BOAA toxin). Vitamin C 500–1000 mg/day antagonises BOAA excitotoxicity.

</details>

### Q118 — Smear-negative TB: next step per NTEP *(2023)*

Both sputum smears negative, CXR suggestive of TB — next step per NTEP?

- A. Repeat sputum smear
- B. CBNAAT (GeneXpert)
- C. Line probe assay
- D. Wait before starting ATT

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. CBNAAT (GeneXpert MTB/RIF)** — For smear-negative presumptive pulmonary TB with clinical/radiological suspicion: CBNAAT detects MTB and RIF resistance simultaneously within 2 hours.

</details>

### Q119 — Retrospective cohort study *(2023)*

Aniline dye workers vs office clerks, exposure and outcome from past employment records — study type?

- A. Retrospective cohort
- B. Prospective cohort
- C. Case-control
- D. RCT

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Retrospective cohort** — Two groups defined by **exposure** (exposed vs unexposed); outcomes from past records → retrospective/historical cohort.

</details>

### Q120 — Blood pressure tracking *(2023)*

Childhood BP predicts adult BP; those with high childhood BP have high adult BP. This is:

- A. Rule of halves
- B. Tracking of blood pressure
- C. STEPwise approach
- D. Primordial prevention

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Tracking of blood pressure** — Tendency for physiological variables to maintain relative rank within a population over time. Forms basis for early childhood hypertension intervention.

</details>

### Q121 — ASHA: counselling and information *(2023)*

Primigravida avoiding fruits due to abortion myths; whose primary duty to counsel?

- A. ANM
- B. Medical officer
- C. Trained birth attendant
- D. ASHA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. ASHA** — Frontline community health activist; resident village woman with ≥10th-grade education, aged 25–45. Addresses community myths and motivates uptake of health services.

</details>

### Q122 — Gray kit for cervicitis (STI) *(2023)*

Young woman with vaginal discharge + cervical erosions on speculum — which NACO syndromic management kit?

- A. Green
- B. Red
- C. Gray
- D. Yellow

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Gray kit** — Cervical discharge/erosions (cervicitis) → gray kit (cefixime + azithromycin) covering gonorrhoea and chlamydia.

</details>

### Q123 — Years of potential life lost (YPLL) *(2023)*

YPLL is attributed to:

- A. Years lost to morbidity
- B. Years lost to premature death
- C. Years lost to disability
- D. Years lost to poor quality of life

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Years lost to premature death** — YPLL = Σ(reference age 75 − age at death) for all premature deaths. Higher in conditions affecting younger people.

</details>

### Q124 — TT/Td at school immunisation *(2023)*

10-year-old school child — which vaccine in school immunisation programme?

- A. Measles
- B. Rotavirus
- C. TT/Td
- D. Hepatitis B

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. TT/Td** — National Immunisation Schedule: TT/Td at 10 and 16 years as school-based boosters.

</details>

### Q125 — Colombo Plan for cancer *(2023)*

Colombo Plan provided assistance for which cancer treatment facility in India?

- A. PET scan units
- B. Human resource development
- C. Linear accelerator units
- D. Cobalt therapy units

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Cobalt therapy units** — Cobalt-60 external beam radiotherapy units set up at Indian medical institutions as part of Colombo Plan (1951) aid.

</details>

### Q126 — BOAA toxin: neurolathyrism *(2023)*

Poor farmer, successive crop failures, spastic paraparesis + UMN signs — responsible toxin?

- A. Aflatoxin
- B. Beta-oxalyl-amino-alanine (BOAA)
- C. Ergot alkaloids
- D. Fusarium toxin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. BOAA (beta-oxalyl-amino-alanine)** — From *Lathyrus sativus* (khesari dal). Causes irreversible UMN disease (neurolathyrism). Consumed during famines/crop failures.

</details>

### Q127 — Tetanus: clean wound, full course 10 years ago *(2023)*

Clean-cut wound, no bleeding, full tetanus vaccination 10 years ago — management?

- A. TIG + full course vaccine
- B. TIG only
- C. Single-dose tetanus toxoid
- D. Full course tetanus toxoid

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Single-dose tetanus toxoid** — Clean wound + fully immunised + >10 years → 1 booster Td only. No TIG required. TIG + full course for unimmunised with tetanus-prone wounds.

</details>

### Q128 — Water sample collection during outbreak *(2023)*

Correct method for water sample collection for bacteriological examination during a disease outbreak?

- A. Collect from already opened tap
- B. Let water flow 1 minute first
- C. Keep container close to tap to avoid spillage
- D. Collect from a gentle stream to avoid splashing

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Gentle stream to avoid splashing** — Splashing from tap can contaminate the sterile container from outside. Maintain a gentle, slow flow while collecting.

</details>

### Q129 — RDA definition *(2023)*

Nutrient intake level meeting requirements of 97–98% healthy individuals?

- A. Adequate intake
- B. Dietary goal
- C. Estimated average requirement
- D. Recommended dietary allowance

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Recommended dietary allowance (RDA)** — RDA = EAR + 2SD (covers ~97.5% of the population). EAR = meets 50% of individuals' needs.

</details>

### Q130 — Chickenpox hostel outbreak management *(2023)*

Boys' hostel: fever + headache + pleomorphic rash sparing palms/soles — management?

- A. Isolate 6 days + acyclovir + VZIG to contacts within 72 hours
- B. Isolate 12 days + acyclovir + VZIG within 48 hours
- C. Isolate 6 days only
- D. Only VZIG

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Isolate 6 days + acyclovir + VZIG within 72 hours** — Varicella: treat with acyclovir; isolate until all lesions crusted (~6 days after onset); VZIG to susceptible contacts within 96 hours (ideally 72 hours).

</details>

### Q131 — IMR: sensitive healthcare indicator *(2023)*

Most sensitive indicator of healthcare availability, utilisation, and effectiveness?

- A. Infant mortality rate
- B. Maternal mortality rate
- C. Immunisation coverage
- D. Under-5 mortality rate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Infant mortality rate (IMR)** — Universally accepted as the most sensitive community health indicator. Reflects nutrition, sanitation, maternal health, immunisation, and socioeconomic conditions. India IMR (2020) = 28/1000 live births.

</details>

---

### Q132 — Dietary cholesterol for CAD prevention *(2021)*

Population-level recommendation for dietary cholesterol to prevent coronary artery disease?

- A. 100 mg/1000 kcal
- B. 200 mg/1000 kcal
- C. 300 mg/day
- D. 400 mg/day

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 100 mg/1000 kcal** — ICMR/Park's PSM recommendation: dietary cholesterol <100 mg per 1000 kcal (equivalent to <300 mg/day in a 3000 kcal diet). Saturated fat <10% total energy. Reduces atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease risk.

</details>

---

### Q133 — WIFS dose for adolescent girls *(2021)*

Weekly Iron Folic Acid Supplementation (WIFS) for girls aged 10–19 years — correct dose?

- A. 60 mg Fe + 100 µg FA
- B. 100 mg Fe + 100 µg FA
- C. 100 mg Fe + 500 µg FA
- D. 60 mg Fe + 500 µg FA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 60 mg elemental Fe + 500 µg FA** — National Iron Plus Initiative / WIFS program: weekly IFA tablet for school-age children and adolescents (6–19 years) contains **60 mg elemental iron + 500 µg (0.5 mg) folic acid**. Given weekly (not daily) to reduce side effects. Supervised in schools.

</details>

---

### Q134 — Generation time *(2021)*

Interval from acquiring infection to time of maximum infectivity?

- A. Communicable period
- B. Incubation period
- C. Generation time
- D. Serial interval

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Generation time** — Time from infection in the primary case to peak infectivity (or to transmission to secondary case). Serial interval = time between symptom onset in successive cases in a chain (used when exact infection time is unknown). Communicable period = entire period when host can transmit the pathogen.

</details>

---

### Q135 — NUHM Urban PHCs: calculation *(2021)*

City population 70 lakh, 30% in slums. How many Urban PHCs (UPHCs) are required under NUHM?

- A. 22
- B. 42
- C. 32
- D. 52

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 42** — NUHM norm: 1 UPHC per 50,000 **slum** population. Slum population = 7,000,000 × 30% = 2,100,000. UPHCs = 2,100,000 ÷ 50,000 = **42**. For non-slum urban areas the norm is 1 UPHC per 1,00,000 population.

</details>

---

### Q136 — De facto census method *(2021)*

"De facto" method of census: data collected based on?

- A. Place of birth
- B. Place of enumeration on census date
- C. Place of employment
- D. Usual place of residence

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Place of enumeration on census date** — De facto = count people where they are physically present on census night. De jure = count people at their usual place of residence regardless of where they are on census night. India follows the **de facto** method. Migrants who are away from home are counted at their current location.

</details>

---

### Q137 — Haemoglobin comparison: statistical test *(2021)*

Comparing mean haemoglobin levels between two independent (unrelated) groups — most appropriate test?

- A. Paired t-test
- B. Unpaired (independent samples) t-test
- C. ANOVA
- D. Chi-square test

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Unpaired t-test** — For comparing means of a **continuous normally distributed variable** (Hb) between **two independent** groups. Paired t-test = same subjects before/after (matched pairs). ANOVA = three or more groups. Chi-square = categorical (not continuous) data.

</details>

---

### Q138 — Lead time bias in cancer screening *(2021)*

Breast cancer screening programme improves 5-year survival but autopsy data show no change in overall mortality — which bias is this?

- A. Berkson bias
- B. Survival bias
- C. Lead time bias
- D. Detection bias

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lead time bias** — Screening detects cancer earlier (lead time), so the interval from diagnosis to death is longer, making survival appear improved even though the natural history of the disease is unchanged. Confirmed by absence of mortality reduction. Berkson bias = hospital admission rate bias in case-control studies.

</details>

---

### Q139 — Nalgonda technique for defluoridation *(2021)*

Nalgonda technique — materials used for defluoridation of drinking water?

- A. Alum + Lime
- B. Gypsum + Charcoal
- C. Alum + Charcoal
- D. Gypsum + Lime

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Alum + Lime** — Nalgonda technique: alum (aluminum sulfate) + lime (calcium hydroxide) added to fluoride-rich water → coagulation/flocculation → fluoride adsorbs onto aluminium floc → settles and is filtered. Community-level defluoridation. Bleaching powder also added.

</details>

---

### Q140 — ICDS program coverage *(2021)*

Child presenting with bowing legs (nutritional rickets). Which government programme specifically addresses nutritional deficiencies in children under 6 years?

- A. ICDS (Integrated Child Development Services)
- B. Mid-Day Meal Scheme
- C. Anemia Mukt Bharat
- D. National Nutrition Deficiency Control Programme

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. ICDS** — ICDS covers **0–6 years**: supplementary nutrition, immunisation, health check-up, referral, pre-school education, nutrition and health education. Mid-Day Meal = 6–14 year school children. NNDCP specifically targets Vitamin A, iodine, and iron deficiencies; ICDS is the broader umbrella.

</details>

---

### Q141 — Cluster RCT vs quasi-experimental *(2021)*

20 PHCs randomly allocated to standard care; 20 PHCs to new community intervention. Randomisation is at the PHC (cluster) level. Study design?

- A. Quasi-experimental
- B. Cluster RCT
- C. Case-control
- D. Cross-sectional

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cluster RCT** — Randomisation at the **cluster** (PHC/village/school) level, not individual. Reduces contamination between intervention and control groups. Quasi-experimental = no randomisation (e.g., before-after, controlled before-after). Cluster RCT requires cluster-adjusted statistical analysis (ICC).

</details>

---

### Q142 — Passive surveillance: COVID death reporting *(2021)*

60-year-old with confirmed COVID-19 dies in hospital. Death is routinely reported to the health department by the hospital 7 days later. Surveillance type?

- A. Active surveillance
- B. Passive surveillance
- C. Syndromic surveillance
- D. Sentinel surveillance

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Passive surveillance** — Healthcare providers **spontaneously report** cases/deaths to the health authority through routine notification (no special action by public health authorities to find cases). Active = health workers actively search for cases. Sentinel = selected high-quality reporting sites.

</details>

---

### Q143 — Anopheles larva identification *(2021)*

Mosquito larva: no siphon tube, has palmate hairs, rests parallel to water surface — species?

- A. Culex
- B. Anopheles
- C. Aedes
- D. Mansonia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Anopheles** — Anopheles larva: **no respiratory siphon tube**; **palmate (star-shaped) hairs** on abdomen; lies **horizontal (parallel) to water surface**. Culex and Aedes larvae: have siphon tube; hang head-down at ~45°. Adult Anopheles: spotted wings; rests at 45° to surface; malaria vector.

</details>

---

### Q144 — Complementary feeding: 7-month infant *(2021)*

Correct complementary feeding recommendation for a 7-month-old infant?

- A. ½–1 katori, 3× daily
- B. ½–1 katori, 4× daily
- C. ½–1 katori, 5× daily
- D. ½–1 katori, 6× daily

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. ½–1 katori, 3× daily** — WHO/IYCF/NIN recommendations: 6–8 months = **2–3 times/day** semi-solid food; 9–11 months = 3–4 times/day; 12–23 months = 3–4 times/day + 1–2 snacks. Breastfeeding continues on demand alongside complementary foods.

</details>

---

### Q145 — Nipah virus: fruit farmer *(2024)*

A fruit farmer presents with fever, respiratory difficulty, and neurological complications. Most likely diagnosis?

- A. Ebola
- B. Bacterial meningitis
- C. Nipah virus
- D. Zika virus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Nipah virus** — Fruit bats (*Pteropus* spp.) are reservoir; humans infected via contaminated fruits or contact with infected pigs/humans. Causes encephalitis + respiratory illness. Outbreaks: Kerala (India), Bangladesh, Malaysia. No specific treatment; mortality ~40–75%. Classified as WHO Priority Pathogen.

</details>

---

### Q146 — OPV: lowest cold-chain storage temperature *(2024)*

Which vaccine is stored at the lowest temperature in the cold chain?

- A. OPV
- B. DPT
- C. Hepatitis B
- D. Rotavirus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. OPV (Oral Polio Vaccine)** — Stored **frozen at −20°C** (or −15 to −25°C) at higher cold-chain levels (regional/district vaccine stores). At PHC/sub-centre level, stored at 2–8°C. DPT, Hep B, Rotavirus stored at 2–8°C and are **damaged by freezing** (freeze-sensitive). Shake test used to detect freeze damage in freeze-sensitive vaccines.

</details>

---

### Q147 — Toxic fumes: immediate community action *(2025)*

After an industrial explosion, toxic fumes are released into the air. What is the immediate protective action residents should take?

- A. Telephone friends and leave immediately
- B. Seal cracks and close all windows and doors
- C. Open windows for maximum ventilation
- D. Use a mask only and continue normal activities

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Seal cracks and close all windows and doors** — **Shelter-in-place** protocol for chemical plume: seal building (tape cracks, close windows/doors) to prevent entry of toxic gas until authorities advise evacuation. Moving through the plume can increase exposure. Essential component of chemical/industrial disaster preparedness.

</details>

---

### Q148 — Skeletal fluorosis: intervention NOT helpful *(2025)*

20-year-old from Andhra Pradesh with bowing of lower limbs and osteoporosis; drinks groundwater, eats rice and jowar. Which intervention will NOT help?

- A. Provide running surface water for drinking
- B. Change the water source
- C. Fluoride supplementation
- D. Add lime and alum to drinking water (Nalgonda technique)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Fluoride supplementation** — This is **endemic skeletal fluorosis** from excess fluoride in groundwater (>1.5 mg/L). Supplementation would worsen the condition. Interventions: change/dilute water source, defluoridation (Nalgonda technique with alum + lime), or supply piped surface water. Ascorbic acid may reduce fluoride absorption.

</details>

---

### Q149 — RCT: correct sequence of steps *(2025)*

Correct sequence of steps in a Randomised Controlled Trial (RCT)?

- A. Follow-up → Manipulation → Assessment → Randomisation
- B. Randomisation → Manipulation → Follow-up → Assessment
- C. Assessment → Randomisation → Follow-up → Manipulation
- D. Manipulation → Assessment → Follow-up → Randomisation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Randomisation → Manipulation → Follow-up → Assessment** — Standard RCT sequence: (1) Recruit eligible subjects; (2) **Randomise** to intervention/control; (3) Apply **manipulation** (intervention); (4) **Follow up** over specified period; (5) **Assess** outcome. Randomisation must precede manipulation to eliminate selection bias.

</details>

---

### Q150 — Evaluation vs monitoring *(2025)*

Which health programme activity measures the degree of achievement of objectives and quality of results?

- A. Planning
- B. Surveillance
- C. Monitoring
- D. Evaluation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Evaluation** — **Evaluation** = assessment of whether programme objectives/targets were achieved (effectiveness, efficiency, relevance, impact). **Monitoring** = continuous tracking of ongoing activities and process indicators (inputs, outputs, coverage). Evaluation is periodic; monitoring is continuous.

</details>

---

### Q151 — HIV + active TB: treatment timing *(2025)*

HIV-positive patient (CD4 = 250 cells/mm³) with active tuberculosis, not yet on ART. Correct approach?

- A. Start ATT first, then initiate ART after 2 weeks
- B. Start ART first, then begin ATT after 2 weeks
- C. Start both ART and ATT simultaneously
- D. Start ATT first, then initiate ART after 8 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Start ATT first, then initiate ART within 2 weeks** — WHO/NACO guidelines: initiate **ATT first**, then add **ART within 2 weeks** (for all CD4 counts). Exception: TB meningitis — delay ART by 4–8 weeks to reduce paradoxical IRIS risk. Earlier ART reduces mortality in severely immunocompromised (CD4 <50).

</details>

---

### Q152 — Vaccine Vial Monitor (VVM): usability *(2025)*

Regarding vaccine vial monitors, vials should be discarded when:

- A. The VVM square is lighter than the surrounding circle
- B. The VVM square colour matches or is darker than the surrounding circle
- C. The vial has been opened once
- D. Temperature has been below 2°C

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. VVM square matches or is darker than surrounding circle** — VVM stages: Stage 1 (square lighter than circle) = use; Stage 2 (square same shade as circle) = use but use FIRST; Stage 3 (square slightly darker than circle) = **DO NOT use**; Stage 4 (square much darker) = **DO NOT use**. VVM measures cumulative heat exposure. Open multi-dose vial policy allows reuse of OPV/Rota/IPV/Hep B/DPT at subsequent sessions if VVM stage 1 or 2 and within 4 weeks.

</details>

*Questions Q153–Q222 sourced from PrepLadder National Health Programs, PrepLadder Levels of Prevention, PrepLadder PSM Clinical/Non-Clinical, and pw.live PSM pages (recall-based, multi-year).*
